# BUSBAR 1.6.0 — THE LEDGER

**There are exactly two documents in this release.**

| file | answers | authority |
|---|---|---|
| `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` | *How is this built?* — Laws, the 78 locked decisions, the seams | **THE SPEC.** Part 1 > Part 3 > Part 2 > Part 5 |
| `1.6.0-LEDGER.md` (this file) | *What is left, and who is on it?* | **THE WORKLIST.** One row per open item |

Everything else under `docs/design/1.6.0-*.md` is **SCRATCH**: an audit writes it, its rows
are folded in here, and **the scratch file is then DELETED**. A scratch file that survives its
fold becomes tomorrow's stale doc that misleads the next agent — that is the disease this
structure exists to cure. Twenty parallel documents is how a release stops knowing what it is.

**ONE EXCEPTION, MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — `1.6.0-TRACKER.md` IS NOT SCRATCH.** It is read by a
gate at run time: `xtask/src/gates/no_deferral.rs:60` pins it as `const TRACKER`, `:310` fails the
`no-deferral:waiver-shape` row outright if it cannot be read, and `:346` requires every waiver's
`expiry` id to resolve to a row **in that file**. Deleting it turns the no-deferral gate red on
every commit. It was listed in THE FOLD in error; it is struck from that list below and stays.

## THE ROW SCHEMA

```
ID | ITEM | WHERE | CLASS | STATUS | OWNER | EVIDENCE
```

* **CLASS** — `money` · `security` · `gate` · `test` · `doc` · `branch` · `engine`
* **STATUS** — `OPEN` · `IN-FLIGHT` · `DONE` · `PARKED-OWNER` · `DEAD`
* **EVIDENCE** — a command, a commit, or a file:line. Never a claim without one.

Ordering is by CLASS: **money first, then security**, then the rest. That is the Laws' order
and it is not negotiable.

### Three rules for every row

1. **A row without EVIDENCE is not a row.** "Looks fine" is not evidence; a command someone
   else can re-run is.
2. **A gap and a failure must never be the same output — and neither may a gap and a success.**
   If a check could not run, its row says `ABSENT`, never `DONE`.
3. **Never self-approve a billed-byte change (#10/#59).** Money defects go to `PARKED-OWNER`
   with cell, diff, root cause and recommendation. The owner signs, not the agent.

### A fourth rule, for this file only: **a copied claim stays a copied claim**

Rows folded from an audit carry that audit's evidence, not this file's endorsement. Where two
audits contradict each other **both** are recorded and the contradiction is flagged in the row.
Where an audit asserted something with no evidence, the row says `EVIDENCE: none given` rather
than inheriting the assertion as fact.

---

## MONEY (Part 1 — supreme; "it is right or it is wrong")

**28 rows — 21 `PARKED-OWNER`, 7 `OPEN`. 0 `DONE`, and that is deliberate: this ledger does not
close a money row. One (M5) is fixed in the tree and parked on the owner's signature, not on work.**

> **THE MONEY CENSUS'S VERDICT, STATED PLAINLY: IT WOULD NOT PASS A BANK AUDITOR.**
> **Read M20, M21, M22, M1, M23 and M27 first** — a computed price sealed into the WAL, a ~0.2% silent
> underbill on every configured rate in every deployment, a `settled` field declared in nano-units
> that carries bytes, a saturating cast with a measured `u64::MAX`-vs-`0` split, and a signed
> back-dated correction that moves the invoice and never the book. Everything else in this table is
> real and none of it is that.
>
> **One auditor's process note, worth more than any single row: it initially judged M20 LAWFUL and
> reversed only after following the arithmetic through four hops.** A field-name census passes
> M20. That is the standard this section is held to.
Money is binary and never self-approved. No agent closes a row in this section.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **An absurd-but-finite rate saturates to `u64::MAX` instead of being refused.** The guard's own comment claims it ensures "never a huge/garbage integer rate"; it does not. Rust float→int casts saturate, so `1e30` passes `is_finite() && v > 0.0` and becomes `u64::MAX` nanos/token. A config typo becomes a catastrophic overcharge. **⚠ A REPORT THAT THE DEFECT HAS TWO HOMES IS WRONG, AND THE COLLATOR MEASURED IT.** One audit reported a second identical defect at `crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs:208`. Re-measured at HEAD: **`:208` is a CALL SITE inside `from_micro_rates_in`, not a second definition**, and the real definition at `rate.rs:40-47` **HAS the clamp** — `if v.is_finite() && v > 0.0 && v <= u64::MAX as f64`. **The drift runs the other way, and that is worse:** the fix exists, it landed on the ledger copy, and it was never carried to the one on the enforcement path. A third audit says the same and names the incident — `crates/busbar-kernel-budget/src/price.rs:59-74` writes it down: *"a clamp for a finite-but-overflowing rate went onto one copy and not the other, and the two answered a config typo with too many zeros as 'nothing' on one side and 'the largest rate there is' on the other."* The budget crate was rewired onto `nano_rate`; **`cost.rs` was not.** **AND THE AGREEMENT TEST IS TAUTOLOGICAL** — it compares `nano_rate` against a `RateNanos` that now delegates to `nano_rate`, and never sees the kernel's copy. So a rate-card typo yields `u64::MAX` nanos/token in the enforcement pricer and `0` in the ledger, and no test can see the disagreement. **MEASURED CONSEQUENCE, no longer hypothetical: at `1e17` µ/unit the door reads `u64::MAX` and the book reads `0`** — verbatim the drift `kernel-budget` claims to have eliminated. | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs:138-145`, reached in production at `cost.rs:446`. **NOT** `busbar-kernel-ledger` — that copy is correct | **PARKED-OWNER** | `fn nanos(utok: f64) -> u64 { let v = (utok*1000.0).round(); if v.is_finite() && v > 0.0 { v as u64 } else { 0 } }` at `cost.rs:138`; `nano_rate` at `rate.rs:40-47` **with** `&& v <= u64::MAX as f64` — re-read by the collator with `sed -n '35,55p' crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs`, and `sed -n '195,225p'` shows `:208` is `.set(currency, nano_rate(micro))`, a call. | M28 | **A SIGNED `amend_rate_history` CAN BE REPLAYED, AND CAN BE DATED INTO THE FUTURE.** **What DID land, verified by reading it:** the ed25519 hardening is fully present — `operator_fingerprint` must equal `sha256(sealed key)`, strict `VerifyingKey` verification over a canonical payload, and the verb is admitted only under `Set`. **What did not:** (a) **no replay guard** — `canonical_amend_payload` signs `busbar/amend-rate-history/v1` + `effective_from`, `effective_until`, `currency`, `per_request_fee`, `reason`, `rates`, with **no nonce, no idempotency key, no sequence**, so a captured, valid, operator-signed correction can be re-POSTed indefinitely, each time appending another entry; (b) **no future-date guard** — the only window check is `effective_until <= effective_from`; **nothing compares `effective_from` to now**, so a signed correction dated forward sits in the history and takes effect silently when the clock reaches it. **Honest scoping: the repriced RESULT of a replay is the same, so (a) is history bloat and audit noise rather than a price change** — the branch nonetheless treated it as a defect worth five tests, and (b) is a real silent-activation path. | `crates/busbar/src/root/units_admin/mod.rs:1060` (`amend_rate_history_effect`), `:1241` (`canonical_amend_payload`) | **PARKED-OWNER** | `idempotency` appears nowhere in `units_admin/mod.rs` except an unrelated comment at `:2507`; `grep -rn "in the future\|future_from\|is_future"` over `crates/busbar/src` and `crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src` returns **nothing**. Fix on branch `m5-amend`: `AlreadyAmended`, `ZeroNonce`, `AmendReceipt`, `PredatesOpening`, and the tests `an_idempotency_key_replay_appends_nothing_and_returns_the_same_receipt`, `a_failed_apply_frees_the_replay_slot`, `a_future_from_is_refused` |

## GATE — instruments, rules and registers

**15 rows — 13 `OPEN`, 1 `DONE`, 1 standing practice.** G1–G5 are properties of the register + judge that make the money rows
above cheaper to create and harder to see; none is an entry defect. G6–G11 are instrument defects
found by other audits. **G6 and G10 are the same disease in two costumes** — a capability that is
never constructed, and a rule whose entries can never be reached. Both report success for having
done nothing, and between them they account for six separate findings in this ledger.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | **The register has ONE `by:` field and edits accrete under it — TEN widenings have landed under an unchanged signature.** There is no per-edit provenance, so an agent's widening reads as the owner's ruling (M7 is the money instance; `2b366e354` claims a "re-stamp" of a signature that is byte-identical before and after). Recommendation: an append-only `amendments: [{date, by, what}]` array, plus a gate that reds a `classes` widening whose newest amendment is not the owner's. | `accepted-differences.json` schema | **OPEN** | `48ea84225` widened `G-1.classes` with `by:` unchanged. Since 2026-09-15 five entries were removed by **fixing** the divergence (`D-2`, two `F-013`s, `F-017`, `O-1`, all in `19550dd90` — fail-closed) and one added (`F-019`); nothing in the file records which change was whose |
| G2 | **`classes` is per-entry, not per-cell** — `entry_may_take` grants the entry's whole allowed set on **every** matched cell, so a rationale that scopes a class to one cell in prose cannot be honoured by the mechanism. Two live instances: F-016's `BOOT-P31` "class `effects.stderr` only" (M14) and D-3's "`ledger\|currency\|minor-unit-rounding` may not be forgiven by it" (M15). Fix is per-cell classes, or splitting the entry. | `run.rs:681-687` | **OPEN** | as cited |
| G3 | **The `changelog-register` gate proves PRESENCE, never TRUTH.** It checks each entry's `changelog` string appears verbatim (whitespace-normalised) in the newest `## [x.y.z]` section; the judge only checks the key is truthy. Re-run under the gate's own anchoring: all 41 lines present. Three of them are false of the shipping code — see DOC1. **A second audit found the sharpest instance:** `D-3` is the only entry carrying an explicit `changelog_reason: null` **beside a non-null `changelog`** — a state its own `_comment` rule does not describe — and its own AUDIT NOTE concludes the changelog claim *"is not true of the endpoint these three cells exercise today"*. `changelog-register-check.sh` tests **presence, never coherence**, so it cannot see this. | `xtask/src/gates/changelog_register.rs`, `normalize()` at `:699-701`; `run.rs:1152`; `scripts/changelog-register-check.sh`; `accepted-differences.json` entry `D-3` | **OPEN** | as cited |
| G4 | **A transform rule that deletes a value from BOTH sides masks a change in that value.** D-1's rule 2 deletes ` diag=BUSBAR-\d{4}` from golden and candidate, so a *changed* diagnostic code is erased on both and reports byte-equal. **Inert today** — zero `BUSBAR-[0-9]` across all 916 goldens — and **live the day the goldens are re-recorded from a coded build.** Report-only; flagged so the re-record does not land silently. | `run.rs:1666-1667`; `accepted-differences.json` entry `D-1` | **OPEN** | as cited |

Nothing is red today because both cells are `SKIP` — but the entries that describe those behaviours do not reach the cells built to demonstrate them. | cells `llm\|responses\|responses\|request\|ok_citation`, `llm\|bedrock\|bedrock\|request\|ok_cachepoint_document` | **OPEN** | cross-checked every `SKIP` cell against `accepted-differences.json` and `accepted-gaps.json`: register `—`, accepted-gaps `—` for both |

The rule to record, in the tree's own words: **A CAPABILITY THAT IS NEVER CONSTRUCTED IS A CAPABILITY THAT DOES NOT SHIP — its tests prove the code works, not that it runs.** A gate is being built for this; `1.6.0-unconstructed-audit.md` is the scratch that will carry it. | new gate, unbuilt | **OPEN** | the three instances: S6 (`signing_with`, 0 non-test callers), M18 (`booked_lines()` → `Vec::new()` in both production impls), `known_missing_forbid` (both auth crates) |

| G8 | **The done-oracle scores an ABSENT-STEP and a FAILURE identically** — a direct breach of this ledger's rule 2. `absent_step()` prints a distinct line (`[RED] <label> — NOT PRESENT YET (<what>)`) and then sets `CUR_RED=1`, exactly as `step()` does on a real failure, so in the script's own final readout a group that *could not run* is indistinguishable from one that ran and failed. A RED is a bug to fix; an ABSENT-STEP is a check to build. | `scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh`, `absent_step()` ~line 344 | **OPEN** | as cited. **Correction to a figure in circulation: the done-oracle's group count is 23, not 24.** `grep -c '^begin_group'` returns 24 because it counts line 93, the `begin_group()` function definition itself; the script's own gating count (`awk '/^begin_group /{n++}'`, with the trailing space) returns 23, `grep -c '^end_group$'` returns 23, and `DONE_GROUPS_DECLARED=23` agrees with the file. There is no missing 24th group |

Measured out-of-band ahead of the umbrella run, so the authoritative row is still the done-oracle's. | `scripts/public-hygiene-lint.py`, tree-wide | **OPEN** | `python3 scripts/public-hygiene-lint.py --root . --quiet` → exit 1, `3466 public file(s) scanned against 11 rules — 79 hit(s), 20 allowed`. ## ENGINE — production code: dead code, duplication, architecture

**26 rows — 26 `OPEN`, 0 `DONE`.** These are the duplication census's ranked worklist. Its 27th row
(two SSRF guards) closed and is carried as **S3**; its rate-clamp row is carried as **M1**; its
SigV4, `CallerToken` and breaker-clamp rows are **S10**, **S11**, **S12**. Ordered by the census's
own tiers: safety/money first, then structural decisions, then clean folds, then design work.

**The census's headline, for context, not as a row:** the excess is **90,667** production lines
(62,956 → 153,623, 2.44×), every line assigned, both columns balancing with zero unassigned files.
**The owner's hypothesis scored: "54 crates" REFUTED (~3–5%, measured); "major duplication"
confirmed in kind and corrected in size — ~12,000–14,000 lines, not 91,000.** 21,620 lines (23.8%)
have **no v1.5.5 ancestor at all** and sit *outside* the four new planes: the plane host, the kernel
unit loop, the WAL, the OAuth2 AS, the diagnostics catalog. Five subsystems everyone assumed had
ballooned did not — admin **1.21×**, config+validation **1.22×**, governance **1.23×**, ingress
**1.29×**, auth **1.40×**. Those honest negatives are worth as much as the positives: chasing them
would have been wasted work.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ENG1 | **Three SSE parsers with the wrong grammar, and the postmortem for that exact production outage is already in the tree.** Two use `str::lines()`, which does not split on a bare CR; one carries a **3-of-9** terminator table. `plane-llm`'s `split_event` additionally *assigns* rather than accumulates `data`, **silently dropping all but the last line of a multi-line payload**. | `crates/busbar-plane-llm/src/codec.rs:266`; `crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/client/transport.rs:414`; `crates/busbar-a2a/src/a2a/grpc.rs:716` | **OPEN** | the written postmortem is at `crates/busbar-substrate-values/src/proxy/sse.rs:124-149`. ## BRANCHES — 816 never merged, ONE ROW EACH

### ⚖ OWNER RULING: EXACTLY TWO PREFIXES. A BRANCH'S NAME IS ITS VERDICT.

| prefix | means | test |
|---|---|---|
| **`delete/*`** | **proven safe to delete.** Deleting it loses nothing. | every distinctive symbol it adds is present in the trunk, **verified** |
| **`todo/*`** | **carries something the trunk lacks.** Names what, so a reader knows why it survives. | anything that is not the above |

**Nothing else.** No `verify/*`, no `land/*`, no `keep-*`, no `wip*`. This **replaces** the earlier
three-way `DELETE` / `MERGE→` / `KEEP` scheme; rows below carry the proposed new name.

> **WHY THE RULING EXISTS, and it is the finding:** today's `delete/*` prefix **asserts something
> untrue at scale.** Four slices in — ~400 branches — and `EMPTY` is **0–2 per slice.** A prefix
> that claims *"safe to delete"* over branches carrying live security fixes is **worse than no
> prefix**, because it invites exactly the bulk deletion it appears to authorise.

**816 rows. 60 → `delete/*` · 756 → `todo/*`** (138 adjudicated, 206 symbol-classified only,
**412 unaudited and fail-closed**).

### ⛔ TWO PRECONDITIONS BEFORE ANY BRANCH IS RENAMED OR DELETED

1. **A BUNDLE NEWER THAN THE AUDIT MUST EXIST — AND IT NOW DOES.**
   `~/Downloads/busbar-all-refs-<today>.bundle`, 65 MB, **2,391 refs**, `git bundle verify` reports
   a complete history. The prior bundle was **Sep 21 and predated everything found today.**
   **No branch is renamed or deleted before a bundle newer than the audit exists.**
2. **READ BR-B1 AND BR-T1 FIRST.** A **161-file** golden corpus and **142** golden recordings exist
   only on branches, and **a 1.6.0 binary cannot regenerate them — 1.5.5 is the reference.** If
   those branches go, the corpus is unrecoverable. Highest deletion risk in all 816.

### HOW TO READ A ROW — the distinction is the entire value of this table

* **`→ delete/*`** — `EMPTY` or `HARVESTED`. Safe.
* **`→ todo/*` · adjudicated** — a `SURVIVOR` the slice **named**: it says what the branch carries
  and why the trunk lacks it. The `carries:` field is that answer.
* **`→ todo/*` · symbol-classified only** — it means *"a name this branch defines is not in the
  trunk"*, which — as the false-positive tables show — **is also true of harvested work whose
  behaviour landed under a different name.** Do not delete; do not schedule as recovery either,
  until someone reads the trunk's copy.
* **`→ todo/*` · fail-closed** — **UNAUDITED.** Nothing is known. `todo/` is the safe default here,
  **not a finding.** Unproven-safe is not proven-unsafe.

> **THE FRAMING THAT KEEPS THIS TABLE HONEST.** `SURVIVOR` is 76–94 of ~103 per slice, so **this
> table must not be read as 750 losses.** A mechanical `SURVIVOR` verdict is a **ranking signal,
> not a defect count** — much of that mass is the `busbar-unit-*` → `busbar-kernel-*` refold
> rewriting prose test names around behaviour that **did** land. That is exactly why *adjudicated*
> and *symbol-classified* are different rows.

**Ancestry and patch-id are both useless here** and no future reader should retry them: only
**3 of 819** branches are ancestors of HEAD (the trunk was rebuilt), and the rename waves moved
every path. **Symbols survive renames; paths do not.**

### THE ADJUDICATED FINDINGS — what the `MERGE` rows actually carry

| ID | finding | where | status | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-B1 | **⛔ A 161-FILE GOLDEN CORPUS THAT CANNOT BE REGENERATED.** The 1.5.5 shadow-oracle midstream-fault goldens exist only on a branch in slice `ab`. A 1.6.0 binary cannot produce them — **1.5.5 is the reference.** Delete the branch and the corpus is gone for good. **Highest deletion risk in the entire 816.** | slice `ab` S10 | **OPEN — KEEP, owner eye before any deletion** | the corpus is 161 files; no 1.6.0 artifact can recreate a 1.5.5 recording |
| BR-S1 | **A REJECTED CONFIG LEAVES ITS PROCESS-WIDE LIMITS INSTALLED.** A config apply that fails to persist installs the limits anyway: `appbuild.rs:474` commits the guard at `:1923` — the last line of `build_app_from_config` — and does not return it, while the admin apply path persists *afterwards*. **THIS IS THE SSRF CLASS EXACTLY: the spelling `InstallGuard::install` IS in the trunk, so a name scan calls it harvested; reading it reverses the answer.** The trunk ships the *sibling* test `..._does_not_rotate_gov_credentials` for the same transaction on a different resource — **so the class was understood and this member was dropped in a fold.** | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/appbuild.rs:474`, `:1923`; `crates/busbar-core-admin/src/v1/json/handlers.rs:1586` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the fix exists on `delete/keep-core-validate`: a `#[must_use] InstalledLimits` moving the commit to `.keep()` after persist **and** swap, plus `test_admin_v1_config_settings_persist_failure_does_not_install_limits` |
| BR-B2 | **The streams plane family is unlanded and these branches are the only copy** — `busbar-plane-streams{,-openai,-gemini,-twilio}`, `busbar-contract-transport`, `api/src/sha256.rs` with RFC-4231 + SigV4 vectors. Largest unlanded body in slice `ab`. **Not optional scenery: #18/#48 make streaming one of the five planes.** Needs a ruling on whether the trunk's `busbar-plane-streaming` supersedes it or whether this is owed work. | slice `ab` S2 | **PARKED-OWNER** | the four crates have no trunk equivalent under those names |
| BR-B3 | **Telemetry export sinks, pulled pending a ruling that never came** — file / webhook / OTLP / Prometheus, including `an_ssrf_refused_target_composes_no_sink` and *"a cloud-metadata target is refused before it can become a sink"*. **The trunk's SSRF guard is intact (`ff9b`, 57 occurrences) — the gap is the sinks, not the guard.** | slice `ab` S3; the pull is recorded at `docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md:866` | **PARKED-OWNER** | as cited |
| BR-B4 | **The `PluginError`/`ErrorClass` catalog exists in ZERO trunk files — found independently by two slices.** 361 lines in `busbar-contract/src/error.rs` plus `plugin-loader/src/{config,policy}.rs`, bounded at **8 params / 256 codes / 16 locales**, with a `developer_message` / user-template split. **The trunk's only `PluginError` hit is a doc line PROMISING it** (`docs/design/1.6.0-one-face-per-kind.md:121`, describing it as something a future STORE-4 "lands"). So the trunk has the plan and none of the code. Security-relevant on two axes: the bounds (an unbounded operator-supplied catalog is a memory-exhaustion surface) and the disclosure split (what a refusal tells the caller). | slices `ab` S4 and `ac` S2 | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | `git grep -lF ErrorClass HEAD` → 0; `developer_message` → 0; `error_catalog` → 0; `default_locale` → 0; `PluginError` → 1 file, a doc line |
| BR-B5 | **Conformance suites are missing for FIVE plugin kinds.** `plugin-testkit` was deliberately deleted (owner ruling 2026-09-22, recorded in `crates/store-memory/Cargo.toml`; #2/#31 forbid a shared test util between plugins) — **but the re-inlining is incomplete.** Counting `*_conformance` files in the trunk by kind: plane 4 + loader 2, store 2, export 1, and **auth 0, transport 0, unit 0, secret 0, hook 0.** The only copies of those five suites are on these branches. **Salvage as per-crate inlined tests, per the ruling — never as a shared crate.** | slice `ac` S3 | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the per-kind counts as given; `crates/plugin-testkit/` correctly does not exist (0 files under that prefix) |
| BR-B6 | **A cost-model two-projection equality proof, and the invariant *"admission and accrual must read ONE pinned cost-model snapshot per request."*** Directly relevant to M20/M21/M22 — cross-reference before ruling on any of them. | slice `ab` S7 | **OPEN — money-adjacent** | the branch carries both the proof and the invariant as named tests |
| BR-S2 | **UNBOUNDED RECURSION IN THE PLUGIN-PACK SCHEMA RESOLVER — operator-supplied input, and the failure is a stack overflow.** The trunk caps the schema **walk** (`scan` carries `depth` and refuses past `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH = 64`, `pack.rs:321`) but not the schema **resolve**: `resolve_effective` (`pack.rs:218`) takes **no depth parameter** and recurses through `$ref` (`:238`) and once per `allOf` member (`:250`) **without ever returning through `scan`**, so `scan`'s counter does not advance for the whole resolve chain. The `resolving: &mut HashSet<String>` guard only catches a `$ref` that **repeats a name** — a chain of N *distinct* `$defs` repeats nothing. The triggering document is **flat**, so `serde_json`'s own parse-recursion limit does not apply. **An abort with no diagnostic is the one answer a validator must not give.** | `crates/plugin-sdk/src/pack.rs:218,238,250,321`; reached via `--settings-schema-file` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the fix on `origin/verify/keep-tests-plugins` adds `depth: u32` + `MAX_RESOLVE_DEPTH = 64` to `resolve_effective` and a **200-link non-cyclic** regression test asserting the refusal comes from the resolver's own cap (`err.contains("while resolving")`), not from `scan`'s |
| BR-S3 | **`$BUSBAR_SIGN_KEY`'s hex error ECHOES A NIBBLE OF THE ed25519 SEED.** `hex::FromHexError::InvalidHexCharacter { c, index }` names the offending character and its index, and that character is a nibble of the 32-byte **signing seed**. A packaging run is exactly where that line ends up — a CI log, a pasted bug report. Narrow (one nibble, malformed key only) — **but it is private key material on a path whose entire purpose is handling private key material.** | `crates/plugin-sdk/src/pack.rs:537` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the trunk builds the refusal as `.map_err(\|e\| format!("{SIGN_KEY_ENV} is not valid hex: {e}"))`. The branch replaces it with a value-free `sign_key_hex_error()` naming only the variable and the required shape |
| BR-S4 | **`100-continue` IS GRANTED BEFORE THE BODY CAP IS CHECKED.** The trunk writes `HTTP/1.1 100 Continue` immediately after the transfer-encoding framing checks; the declared `Content-Length` is **not** compared against `request_body_max_bytes` first — that check lives further down in the body-reading branch. So a request declaring a length past the cap is told to go ahead and upload a body the transport has **already decided to refuse.** **`curl` sets `Expect: 100-continue` itself for any body past about a kibibyte, so this is the ordinary path, not a crafted one.** | `crates/busbar-transport-http/src/lib.rs:1152` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the **chunked** arm is fine and was harvested — `if read_so_far > max_body_bytes { return Err(TransportError::Framing); }` is checked before the done-test |
| BR-S5 | **The LLM plane claims the bare model-LISTING route.** `PathSeg::Tail` swallows whatever remains **including nothing**, so `V1_MODELS` and `V1BETA_MODELS` claim `/v1/models` and `/v1beta/models` — the bare listings, which name no model, carry no request body and have no operation class in this plane. The plane then names a dialect for a target it cannot serve, tries to read a request document out of a body that has none, and answers in a dialect's envelope where the listing's own answer belonged. | `crates/busbar-plane-llm/src/claims.rs:81` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the fix on `origin/verify/keep-registry-pin` inserts `PathSeg::Var` before the tail in both constants, updates the registry golden in `crates/busbar/src/root/registry.rs`, and adds the regression test |
| BR-S6 | **No refusal for a configured-but-missing plugins directory, and the EPHEMERAL memory-store default ships SILENTLY.** A config with `plugins.enabled: true` and a `plugins.dir` that does not exist boots — or passes `--validate` — with no refusal naming the setting, the condition, or the **resolved** path. | boot validation | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | verified absent over HEAD: `BOOT-142` → **0 files** (its sibling `BOOT-W14` is in 6); `plugins.enabled is true` → 0; `auto-floored` → 0; `posture banner` → 0; `store-ephemeral-posture` → 0. `is not a directory` → 1 file, an unrelated symlink-traversal doc comment at `crates/plugin-loader/src/tests/stage_tests.rs:108`. The branches add the refusal, resolve a relative `plugins.dir` against the **config file's** directory rather than the process CWD, and add a one-shape posture banner covering all three postures |
| BR-S7 | **`busbar_contract::kinds::Secret` HAS NO IMPLEMENTOR ANYWHERE IN THE TRUNK — the contract's secret face is declared and dead.** The trait declares `ref_grammar`, `resolve`, `watch`, `sign`, `seal`, `unseal`. `crates/plugin-loader/src/lib.rs:1437` implements `busbar_api::SecretModule for DynSecret` with **`resolve` only** — `watch`, `sign`, `seal` and `unseal` have **no path to a loaded secret plugin.** | `crates/busbar-contract/src/kinds.rs:492`; `crates/plugin-loader/src/lib.rs:1437` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | `git grep 'ref_grammar' HEAD -- crates` → **exactly one line, the trait declaration itself**; `git grep 'impl .*Secret for' HEAD -- crates` lists `SecretModule`, `SecretSource`, `CheckpointSecret` — **never `impl Secret for …`**. Two branches wire it, with `SECRET_LANE_KEY = "cold"`, a `SECRET_REF_GRAMMAR`, and the single workspace-wide wire-token ↔ face-error map for the frozen five `SecretErrorKind` tokens, so **a denial, a miss and an outage stay distinct rather than collapsing to one refusal** |
| BR-M1 | **THE TARIFF / PRICING SUBSYSTEM IS ABSENT FROM THE TRUNK IN ITS ENTIRETY — and a user-facing migration note is written as though it shipped.** `git grep -il 'tariff' HEAD` over the whole repository returns **three files, all documentation.** There is **no source.** Yet `docs/migration-1.6.md:97` ships the behaviour claim *"A configured voice/streams `session_fee` now draws… on 1.6.0 it charges once per session, exactly as configured"*, and `docs/design/1.6.0-mcp-engine.md` twice names `kernel::tariff_cell` as an existing walk target. **The single most consequential missing piece is the ROUNDING RULE**, declared on the branch in the contract so every side reads one answer: `enum Rounding { Bankers, Up, Down }` with **`Bankers` (half-to-even) the default because it is the only one of the three with no bias — rounding up is the house taking a systematic cut of every fraction, rounding down is the customer taking it back** — plus a division-by-zero guard and integer arithmetic throughout (*"there is no floating point on any path that decides money"*). **Whether this was deliberately deferred or dropped in a fold is the open question; the migration note means the owner must answer either way.** | six branches (`keep-tariff-2/-3c/-4/-5/-5b/-7`); `docs/migration-1.6.md:97` | **PARKED-OWNER** | verified absent in the trunk: `enum Rounding` → **0 files**, `Bankers` → 0, `half to even` → 0, `PriceCard` → 0, `price_card` → 0. The trunk's `crates/busbar-kernel-budget/src/price.rs` prices a **different** thing — per-model per-token nano rates — with no notion of a dated price card, a visit/transaction count, a declared currency or a declared rounding direction. Cross-reference **M2**, **M20**, **M21** |
| BR-M2 | **A money-API change the tracker believes SHIPPED did not reach the trunk.** The branches' TRACKER marks G10 (money-c) `[x]` done: `/api/v1/admin/ledger/totals` was to serve quantities-by-declared-class plus `settled_micros` (the **book's balance**) beside `priced_micros` (derived at read time), with `priced_nanos` **leaving** the body. The trunk's `render_totals` emits `bucket, day, lane, provider, priced_nanos, priced_micros, fee_count` — **no `settled_micros`, no `quantities`, and `priced_nanos` is still there.** No arithmetic is wrong today; the gap is that the figure the budget was **actually enforced on** is not served beside the derived price — the exact distinction operators need when a rate row has been added since. **Worth an owner decision rather than a silent re-land.** | `crates/busbar/src/root/units_admin/mod.rs:1491-1513`; slice `ac` S4 | **PARKED-OWNER** | `git grep -lF settled_micros HEAD` → 0; `residual_nanos` → 0 — **but `residual_nanos`'s absence is a RENAME, not a loss** (`busbar_kernel_ledger::identity::residual` / `Residual` / `row_residual` / `reconcile` in `crates/busbar/src/root/ledger_identity.rs`). The branch's `docs/openapi-1.6.0-additive.json` declares `required: [bucket, day, lane, provider, quantities, fee_count, settled_micros, priced_micros]` |

Cheap and worth re-landing. | `crates/busbar-core/src/auth/tests/keys_arm_view_tests.rs` (97 lines, 4 tests) | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | fix at `crates/busbar-kernel-identity/src/chain.rs:145-146` — `self.chain.is_empty() && !self.keys_in_chain`, with `has_no_modules()` split out and documented *"NOT the open door"*. `git grep -lF keys_arm_view_tests HEAD` → 0 |

The branch's helper was renamed in the trunk (`read_u32_le` → `read_len_prefixed_bytes`), which is why a name scan misses this |
| BR-S12 | **Three MCP security claims with no counterpart spelling in the trunk — FLAGGED AS STRONG-SUSPICION, NOT CONFIRMED.** (a) an SSRF-blocked call must land in the call log as **refused** rather than **dispatched**, or the audit trail under-reports blocked egress; (b) an unresolvable secret must name the *server* to the caller and the *source* to **nobody** (error-message secret-source disclosure); (c) an oversized OAuth token-endpoint body must be refused rather than buffered whole (unbounded-read DoS). **The audit says plainly these were NOT opened line-by-line the way its other four gaps were — verify before acting.** Recorded at that confidence rather than promoted. | branch `mcp-legacy-p1` (subset in `mcp-mediums`); `crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/client/issue.rs` | **OPEN — verify first** | HEAD has an unrelated oversized-length guard at `crates/busbar-mcp/src/record.rs:112`, but **nothing on the token endpoint.** Absent: `a_call_the_ssrf_guard_blocked_is_recorded_as_refused_and_not_as_dispatched`, `a_secret_that_cannot_resolve_names_the_server_to_the_caller_and_the_source_to_nobody`, `an_oversized_token_endpoint_body_is_refused_rather_than_buffered_whole`, `classify_send_failure` |
| BR-X1 | **THE POSITIVE-CONTROL RULE PAID FOR ITSELF THREE TIMES IN ONE NIGHT, AND IT BELONGS HERE AS A STANDING PRACTICE.** Three instruments returned confident, wrong answers and **every one was caught only because the auditor ran the check against a case whose answer was already known.** (1) `grep -owF -f` returned **zero** matches for `key_handle` on a 58 MB corpus where `grep -oF` returned **43** — ugrep 7.8.4's `-w` misbehaves at that input size though it is correct on a small file; redone with `-oF`, **seven** symbols moved absent → present. (2) `git diff --pathspec-from-file` is not accepted by this git for `diff`: it exited **129** with a usage message on stderr and **empty stdout**, which read as *"identical to HEAD"* for **all 95 branches** in one slice. (3) A `grep -E 'a\|b'` alternation was spelled wrong and returned a false zero on constant-time credential compare. **Two further instrument failures in a fourth slice:** `rg` in this environment is a **zsh function, not a binary on `PATH`**, so a `#!/bin/bash` helper silently found zero matches for everything and the first pass reported **100% of symbols missing**; and in zsh `"$b:testing/..."` applies the `:t` history modifier and eats the colon, reporting `NO-FILE` for eight branches whose files were plainly present. **THE RULE: a check that reports the same answer for every input has not measured anything. Run it against one case whose answer you already know, every time.** | the audit method itself | **STANDING PRACTICE** | five instrument failures, five known-answer controls, five catches |
